happens: the script loops internally (`sleep $INTERVAL` at `:217`) and EVERY fire path is `exit 0` — which kills the invocation and DESTROYS that state.** The runner restarts it, the counters reset to | 2026-09-07 | **A COMMIT SUBJECT BEGINNING WITH `#` IS SILENTLY DISCARDED WHEN THE COMMIT IS COMPLETED BY `rebase --continue` — and the BODY's first line is promoted into its place, so the log looks fine.** Commit `335102d3`'s intended subject was *"#892 NO GO: the fix reproduced its own ticket's defect one level up"*. The log shows *"A green PROVISIONED writes a manifest…"* — the second paragraph. **Nothing errored and nothing warned.** **The precise mechanism, measured rather than assumed:** `git commit -F -` uses cleanup mode `whitespace`, which does NOT strip comment lines — which is why `#890 r2 GO-with-findings` (c1c04e37) and `#889 Finding 2 closed` (849b94c2) kept their subjects earlier the same day. **`rebase --continue` uses the EDITOR path, whose default cleanup DOES strip them.** Proven with a positive control: `git stripspace --strip-comments` on `#test subject\n\nbody line` returns `body line` alone. **So the same message survives one path and is mutilated by the other**, which is why it took a Secuura-style PR number to expose it. **How it was found is the other half:** Wednesday grepped the daily note for a phrase, got **0 hits**, and — instead of concluding the note was lost — **ran a control grep for a phrase it knew was there.** The control returned 1, so the instrument was fine and the search string was wrong. **That control is what turned a false alarm into a real finding**, and it is the same discipline that failed earlier today when `ls` and `head` were used to prove an absence. **The rule: never begin a commit subject with `#`** — put the PR number after a word (`PR #892 NO GO: …`) — **or pass `--cleanup=verbatim`.** **The general form, which is the part worth keeping: a message passed through a tool that INTERPRETS it can lose content with no error, and the loss is invisible because what remains still reads correctly.** Same family as the unquoted heredoc executing backticks and the `-q` flag suppressing the discriminating line. | correction | w+1 (prose destroyed in transit by a tool that interprets it) | [[2026-08-29_unquoted-heredoc-executes-backticks]] · [[2026-08-07_a-check-that-cannot-fail]] (the control that found it) | rule only; `335102d3`'s subject is not recoverable and history was NOT rewritten for a cosmetic loss |

leg (a) runs first so a repeat cannot mask a real wake; and not editing this file while something depends on it is still right, now better founded because the fix is not the one-liner either row called it. **Handled instead by making the triage CHEAP** — the prescribed order collapsed into one combined command (inbound-only inbox + detector + shell count), rather than three calls per alert. **Wednesday is at 72% and a 75-minute alert storm at 1-2% per triage is a real path into the rotation band on noise alone**, which is the honest reason this is recorded rather than shrugged at. | w | Meaning | Required response |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Isolated** — could be noise | Log it. Write a lesson file only if it would change future behaviour (importance filter). |
| 2 | **Reinforced** — a pattern, not noise | Lesson file mandatory. If one existed and didn't fire, diagnose *why* (wrong file? too abstract? bad retrieval handle?) and fix the lesson, not just the mistake. |
| ≥3 | **Regression** — the system is failing to learn | Treat like a failing test (no-skip rule). Automatic promotion candidate: move the rule into `identity/` or into *enforcement* (launcher check, hook, CLAUDE.md line). Raise it with Kam at the next briefing. |

| 2026-09-04 | ✅ **PRAISE — Kam, panel, on the learning capture that followed his own criticism (verbatim): *"Let's capture anything you think relevant for learnings, as this is an evolutionary process and quite a good one"* then *"That's great. Thank you."*** **THE BEHAVIOUR BEING REWARDED IS NOT THE CAPTURE — it is what came immediately before it: he criticised the session's quality, and the reply CLASSIFIED the errors rather than listing them, named what had held as well as what had not, and REFUSED the available excuse ("it's my context window") because it was unmeasured.** He moved from *"a lack of consistency or thought"* to *"quite a good one"* inside two exchanges, and the thing that moved him was a diagnosis he could check. **PROTECT: (1) when criticised, classify before apologising — a pattern is an answer, a list is not; (2) name what HELD alongside what failed, because a report that only lists faults is as miscalibrated as one that hides them; (3) never spend a plausible excuse on an occasion where it is unmeasured; (4) turn the exchange into a filed lesson in the same session, which is what he asked for and what he called evolutionary.** **THE RISK THIS ROW GUARDS AGAINST: praise for a good response to criticism must not become a reason to seek the criticism, nor to perform contrition. What he rewarded was accuracy under scrutiny, and the accurate answer that night happened to include "most of this was mine" — on a night where it were not, the same discipline would require saying so.** | praise/positive-signal | — (positive signal; frequency-weighted per the 2026-08-03 rule — third explicit praise of a correction-culture behaviour this week) | [[2026-09-04_decisions-held-narration-drifted]] (the lesson he asked for) · [[2026-08-03_frequency-weighted-reinforcement]] (repeated praise = a behaviour to protect) · [[2026-08-21_challenge-me-when-you-think-im-wrong]] (his grant, running Kam→Wednesday this time) · [[2026-08-07_we-each-have-strengths]] | Filed same session, as he asked |

| 2026-09-04 | 🔴 **w=2 (same session, same subject — THE SECRET-SCAN IN WEDNESDAY'S OWN WRAP ROUTINE REPORTS AND DOES NOT REFUSE, and its PREDICATE matched Wednesday's own prose): minutes after leaking a token, the pre-commit check ran as `git diff --cached | grep -c 'github_pat_' || echo "0 — clean"` — it printed **1** and **the commit proceeded anyway**, because the `||` only fires when grep FAILS and nothing was gated on the result.** **A check that reports a hit and does not stop the action is not a gate; it is a log line.** And the shape is the day's own theme: the guard was written in the same breath as the thing it guards and inherited its author's attention rather than its own logic. **THE SECOND DEFECT IS THE PREDICATE: `github_pat_` matched WEDNESDAY'S OWN LEDGER PROSE** — the row describing the incident contains the literal string `github_pat_…` as documentation. **So the alarm fired on the write-up of the leak, not on the leak.** A scanner whose pattern matches its own incident report will cry wolf on exactly the commits that discuss security, which is the set most likely to be waved through. **RESOLVED CORRECTLY, with a control: the real token BODY (the characters after the prefix) returns ZERO in the working tree and ZERO in `git log -S` across all history — and the same search returns 1 on a deliberately planted scratch file, so the zeros are measured rather than empty.** **Nothing secret is committed; only the prose is.** **THE RULES: (1) a secret scan gates or it is decoration — `if grep -q …; then echo REFUSING; exit 1; fi`, never a bare count with a `||`. | 2026-09-04 | 🔴🔴 **w=1 SEVERITY-HIGH (NEW costume — WEDNESDAY PRINTED A LIVE CREDENTIAL INTO ITS OWN OUTPUT, from a guard written to AVOID doing exactly that): checking whether a project's `.env` held a GitHub token, Wednesday wrote a presence check as `echo "token set: ${T:+yes}${T:-no}"`.** **`${T:+yes}` yields `yes` when set — correct. `${T:-no}` yields THE VALUE when set, and `no` only when UNSET.** Concatenated, the line printed `yes` immediately followed by the **full live `github_pat_…` personal access token**. **The intent of the line was explicitly to avoid printing the value — the vault rule is `read the key, never echo it` — and the construct chosen to express that intent is the one that prints it.** **BLAST RADIUS, measured not assumed:** the value reached **this session's transcript and tool-result logs only**, under `/Users/kam_code/.claude/projects/` — **machine-local home, verified OUTSIDE `/Volumes/DevMASTER`, so the drive sync running at that moment could not carry it.** Grep of the whole brain and `2_Project_Files` tree: **zero occurrences.** `git log -S` on Wednesday's repo: **zero.** Nothing staged, nothing committed, nothing mailed, nothing written to a project file. **The exposure is real and it is bounded to the local transcript.** **TREATED AS COMPROMISED REGARDLESS — Kam told immediately and asked to ROTATE the token**, because a credential that has been rendered once is a credential whose confidentiality can no longer be asserted, and 'it only went to a local log' is a claim about reach that does not survive the next backup, screenshot or paste. **The severity is not the measured reach; it is that the reach argument is the one an incident always makes.** **THE RULE, cheap and absolute: NEVER interpolate a secret variable into an echo, in ANY form, including a presence check. Where the distinction could matter — anything reading as an approval, a ruling, or a scope change — resolve it against the panel or the mail before acting, and NEVER let a board comment stand as Kam's authorisation.** **The right fix is upstream and it is Kam's to make, not Wednesday's: agents should write under their own Linear identity so the actor field means something. ## Archived 2026-09-05 08:1x — rows dated 2026-09-02 (52 rows) moved under CLAUDE.md rule 3c (Kam-ruled 2026-09-04 08:32, card wed-ledger-boot-cost: rows older than ~3 days move here verbatim; conservation asserted). Newest-first.

| Date | What happened (root cause) | Type | w | Lesson | Status |

| 2026-09-02 | **FLEET INSIGHT (QA agent, through-code pass on Secuura s110 — two Blockers of opposite kinds, and the pairing is the lesson): (1) #788 — a builder wrote the TESTS for `document_type` erasure and not the fix, and its STATUS claimed "Suite 18 passed" — a suite asserting an absent fix cannot be green, so the record claim is false (the 09-01 'prose ahead of the tests' class, now with the tests themselves ahead of the code); (2) #790 — a builder measured a constraint (the globally unique hash), reasoned from it correctly, rejected a reviewer's wrong fix-shape, and applied the result one SCOPE LEVEL too wide (tenant where the sibling resolver asserts organisation) — "the more dangerous, because everything around it reads as rigorous". The keeper for my briefs: when a sibling mechanism exists for the same relationship, the brief names it and asks for its scope to be mirrored or the difference stated — s110's brief said 'measure the fix-shape at the callee' and nothing about the sibling; the QA found the sibling because it asked what else resolves this relationship. Also: a refusal that reaches the caller as a 5xx is a retry loop (finding 3). Credited at SCORE (s110 0.75, QA 1.0); #790's GO WITHHELD over Peter's at-head approval — the first time tonight the gate overrode a human approval, and the reason is a cross-organisation erasure path. | praise/insight | — | [[2026-08-07_a-check-that-cannot-fail]] (a test that asserts what the code does not do) · [[2026-08-16_classification-is-the-field-that-grants-authority]] (scope is the field) · [[2026-09-01_qa-gate-before-my-verification]] (the gate overriding an approval) | credited at SCORE; the sibling-scope line into the brief template at consolidation |
| 2026-09-02 | **FLEET (Secuura s111, boot — caught by Claude Code's own guard, not by the agent or by me): its reviews-sweep instrument ran `rm $D/*.json` with a possibly-empty `$D`; the harness stopped it with `Dangerous rm operation on possibly-empty variable path — Do you want to proceed?` even under --dangerously-skip-permissions, and the pane sat at the dialog ~5 min until I found it (the wake watcher does not see a dialog as 'idle at prompt' — a WATCHER GAP: a permission dialog is a stall shape the idle leg cannot see). Answered NO from my seat (never-delete: cleanup is a move into quarantine or a fresh dated dir, never `rm`); ADDENDUM + tap. The brief's HOLDS carried 'never delete — quarantine' and the agent still reached for rm in a scratch step — the rule is in memory, not in the path. Enforcement candidates: (1) the watcher's idle leg recognises 'Do you want to proceed?' / '❯ 1. Yes' as a STALL and wakes me; (2) the brief template's standing line gains the explicit costume: 'a cleanup step that can rm on a variable path is written as a move, or not written'. | correction (fleet) | — (family record) | [[2026-08-26_never-delete-cleanup-means-quarantine]] · [[2026-08-09_an-enforcement-you-must-arm-is-not-one]] (a watcher that cannot see the stall shape) · [[2026-09-02_the-statusline-is-the-context-instrument]] (WED-140's sibling: a pane state the watcher cannot name) | dialog answered NO 23:5x; ADDENDUM sent; watcher gap → WED ticket at the wrap |
| 2026-09-02 | **FLEET CORRECTION (Secuura s110, self-disclosed — a push that VOIDED a live approval it had not checked for; the second tonight after #776/s108): Peter approved #786 at `d600d3679` 13:06:23Z while s110 was fixing F-4 on that head; s110's reviews sweep ran before the RE-REQUEST (too late — the approval was already void at the push) and it pushed `a4a513e41` over it. The push was RIGHT (F-4 is a Major on the verdict that sends an operator at the live registry); the fault was not knowing versus knowingly superseding — owned on the PR in its own comment. Its process fix adopted fleet-wide as a standing line: sweep the reviews endpoint BEFORE every push to a PR; an approval that lands mid-work is voided by the next push — read first, supersede knowingly, say so. A third instance → enforcement (a pre-push hook that reads the reviews endpoint for the PR of the current branch). Also from the same STATUS, two catches worth the record: a data-side control that AGREED WITH THE NULL (collision count 0, control 0) answered from the CONSTRAINT instead; and a Major whose fix-shape a REVIEWER prescribed (Peter's tenant FILTER) measured and declined for an assert-and-refuse — the QA's F-1 lesson applied to a human reviewer the same night. | correction (fleet) | — (family record; 2nd instance) | [[2026-08-16_a-recorded-blocker-is-not-a-boundary]] (a review state is a claim with a timestamp) · [[2026-08-14_i-read-representations-they-read-sources]] (Peter holds N ≠ the review state of N, w=60 — this is its push-side twin) | standing line in the next briefs; enforcement at the third |

Plus a REGRESSION that is the mirror of the fix: R4-3 correctly gave first-run-setup the theme and nobody darkened the page (41/67 lines at min 1.09 — readable in light before the round). Also: the tester's near-miss — a `checkValidity()` matrix returning all-true is equally 'no constraint' and 'inert probe'; `willValidate`/`disabled` checked, then a NEGATIVE control (`zzz`, cannot match by construction) proved the constraint absent before the finding was written. Counter-move adopted as the standing acceptance per UI round: one real-engine sweep (dark mode on every page, every visible text line ≥4.5:1; `checkValidity()===false` for a known-bad string; light unhurt).** | praise/insight | — | [[2026-08-07_a-check-that-cannot-fail]] (an all-true matrix is a suspect; a test compiling with the wrong dialect) · [[2026-08-06_local-proof-is-not-target-evidence]] (jsdom + Node regex are local proofs about a browser) · [[2026-08-07_enumerate-every-surface-before-done]] (the page that received the theme and not the styling) · [[2026-09-01_qa-gate-before-my-verification]] (fifth pass, fifth catch above a green suite) | scoreboard 1.0; round 5 → s14 with the v-flag test + the sweep as acceptance; NO deploy from 1f41edc |
